==================================================

2. `density/3`: A predicate to describe the density of a material.

2. `steel_ball` is made_of_steel.
3. `hard_rubber_ball` is made_of_hard_rubber.
4. `table_tennis_ball` is made_of_table_tennis_material.

#### Rules (Horn Clauses / Implications)

   ```

```
2. Glass and hard rubber have densities less than or equal to 1000 kg/m³:

   ```
density(glass_marble, glass, _).
density(hard_rubber_ball, hard_rubber, _).
```

==================================================
### Predicates:
1. has_function_similar_to_animal_skeleton(X, Y)
2. is_part_of_plant_cell(X)

### Facts (Ground Truths):
1. cell_wall(is_part_of_plant_cell).
2. chloroplast(is_part_of_plant_cell).

### Rules (Horn Clauses / Implications):
1. has_function_similar_to_animal_skeleton(cell_wall, animal_skeleton) :- is_part_of_plant_cell(cell_wall), 
   function(cell_wall, structural_support), 
   similar_function(animal_skeleton, structural_support).
2. has_function_similar_to_animal_skeleton(chloroplast, photosynthesis) :- is_part_of_plant_cell(chloroplast),
   function(chloroplast, photosynthesis).

### Note:
The rules above are based on the assumption that a cell wall provides structural support and chloroplasts perform photosynthesis in plant cells. The similarity between these functions and those of an animal skeleton (structural support) has been inferred using logical implications.

Please note that this is not an exhaustive list; additional predicates, facts, or rules may be extracted depending on further analysis.

 LLM ANSWER:

### Extracted Evidence:

#### Predicates:
1. `investigation` - A collection of human inquiry where a deeper understanding is sought about some Thing.
2. `fire_investigation` - Analysis of fire-related incidents, determining origin and cause.
3. `scientific_observation` - Active acquisition of information from a primary source using senses or scientific methods.

#### Facts (Ground Truths):
1. Janette conducts an investigation to see which foods make her feel more fatigued.
2. She eats one food each day at the same time for four days and then records how she feels.
3. Carmen is asked by Janette to do a similar investigation.

#### Rules (Horn Clauses / Implications):
1. `investigation` implies `gathering_information`.
2. `fire_investigation` subclass_of `dangerous_activity`, implying risk of harm or injury during the process.
3. `scientific_observation` requires use of senses or scientific methods for information acquisition.

### Atomized Compound Sentences:
- Janette eats one food each day at the same time and then records how she feels, which can be broken down into separate facts: 
  - She eats a different food each day (predicate).
  - The foods are eaten at the same time every day.
  - Her feelings after eating are recorded.

### Constants vs. Variables:
- Specific entities like Janette and Carmen should be treated as constants (lowercase), while general classes or unknown subjects remain variables (Uppercase).

- **protein_synthesis**: The process whereby biological cells generate new proteins; it is balanced by the loss of cellular proteins via degradation or other processes.

### Facts (Ground Truths):
- Proteins are synthesized in ribosomal subunits.
- Post-translational modifications occur to these proteins during or after protein biosynthesis.
- These modified proteins undergo packaging and direction towards their appropriate destinations.

==================================================

1. `cleared`: A predicate indicating the clearing action on a geographical region.
2. `tropicalRainforest`: A predicate describing the type of forest being cleared.
3. `acreage`: A predicate representing the area affected by the clearing, measured in acres.
4. `yearlyRate`: A predicate specifying that the clearing occurs annually.

### Facts (Ground Truths):
1. `50 million`: The specific number of acres cleared each year.
2. `tropicalRainforest(Region)`: A fact stating that tropical rainforests are being cleared, where Region is a variable representing any geographical region affected by this action.
3. `cleared(tropicalRainforest, 50 million)` : Fact combining the clearing predicate with its arguments.

### Rules (Horn Clauses / Implications):
1. `clearing(TropicalRegion) :- tropicalRainforest(TropicalRegion), cleared(TropicalRegion, Acreage)` 
   - This rule implies that if a region is identified as having tropical rainforests and has been cleared, then it can be inferred to have undergone clearing.

2. `yearlyClearance(Amount) :- yearlyRate(ClearingAction), Amount = 50 million`
   - This rule states that the amount of land being cleared annually (Amount) equals a specific value if the clearance is part of an annual rate (ClearingAction).

### Predicates:
- **reproductive_process**: A biological process that directly contributes to the production of new individuals by one or two organisms.
- **inherited_genetic_material**: The genetic material inherited from parents.

### Facts (Ground Truths):
- **cat** is a type of animal.
- Cats are capable of reproduction.
- Reproduction involves combining and mixing genetic traits through gametes.

### Rules (Horn Clauses / Implications):

1.  If an organism undergoes reproductive process, then it will produce new individuals with inherited genetic material from the parents.
2.  All cats that reproduce have a chance to pass on their characteristics to their offspring.
3.  Cats can inherit traits such as coat color or patterns.

### Extraction Constraints:
- Atomization: Compound sentences containing "and", "or", or "but" are broken into separate facts or rules.
- Constants vs. Variables: Specific entities (names, distinct objects) are treated as constants (lowercase), while general classes or unknown subjects are treated as variables (Uppercase).

- `TransientResponseCharacteristic` is a subclass of SignalProcessing

- A transient response is not necessarily tied to any specific equilibrium or steady state.

### Rules (Horn Clauses / Implications):

1.  If `X` is a subclass of `Y`, then every instance of `X` is also an instance of `Y`.
    ```prolog
subclass_of(X, Y) :- X =< Y.
```

2.  Each instance of TransientResponseCharacteristic implies that it involves processing a signal in electrical engineering and mechanical engineering.
    ```
instance_transient_response_characteristic_implies_signal_processing :-
        transient_response_characteristic_instance,
        signal_processing_definition.
```

==================================================
 LLM ANSWER:
==================================================
### Predicates:
- cracking(X) : X becomes cracked.
- becoming(X, Y): X changes into Y.
- separating(X, Y): Two or more areas of object X are separated from one another.

### Facts (Ground Truths):
- A claw is a part of an animal's body that can be used for hunting by cracking open something. 
- An animal can hunt using its claws to crack open shells and bones but not houses.
- Animals have the ability to use their physical attributes like claws, teeth, or other parts to kill prey.

### Rules (Horn Clauses / Implications):
- If an object is cracked then it has become separated into two areas. 
  - cracking(X) -> separating(X).
- An animal can hunt by using its claw if the thing being hunted is a shell.
  - hunting(Animal, Shell) <- having_claw(Animal), type_of_shell(Something).

==================================================
 LLM ANSWER:
==================================================
### Predicates:
- cracking(X) : X becomes cracked.
- becoming(X, Y): X changes into Y.
- separating(X, Y): Two or more areas of object X are separated from one another.

### Facts (Ground Truths):
- A claw is a part of an animal's body that can be used for hunting by cracking open something. 
- An animal can hunt using its claws to crack open shells and bones but not houses.
- Animals have the ability to use their physical attributes like claws, teeth, or other parts to kill prey.

### Rules (Horn Clauses / Implications):
- If an object is cracked then it has become separated into two areas. 
  - cracking(X) -> separating(X).
- An animal can hunt by using its claw if the thing being hunted is a shell.
  - hunting(Animal, Shell) <- having_claw(Animal), type_of_shell(Something).

==================================================
 LLM ANSWER:
==================================================
### Predicates:
1. originating_in_something(X, Y): X originates in Y.
2. raw_material(X): X is a raw material.

### Facts (Ground Truths):
1. manure from a field is an example of something that comes directly from a source.
2. cats are not typically considered to be raw materials.
3. birds from a nest can be seen as originating in the nest, but it's more accurate to say they come out or leave their nests.

### Rules (Horn Clauses / Implications):
1. coming_directly_from_source(X): X comes directly from its source if and only if there is an instance of raw_material(Y) where Y = X.
2. originating_in_something(X, Y), raw_material(Z), Z = X → coming_directly_from_source(Z).

### Rules (Horn Clauses / Implications):
1. If heat is applied to something, then a chemical reaction may take place.
	* Rule: applying_heat(X) -> possible_chemical_reaction(X).
2. Warming coffee in a microwave causes it to become warm.
	* Rule: warming_coffee(coffee, microwave) -> becoming_warm(coffee).

Note that the rules are based on logical implications and do not necessarily reflect causal relationships between events.

### Atomization:
The compound sentence "If you add heat to something, a chemical reaction may take place" has been broken down into two separate facts/rules:

1. applying_heat(X)
2. possible_chemical_reaction(X)

These have then been used as the basis for further rules and implications.

 LLM ANSWER:
==================================================
### Predicates:
1. `revolves_around`: The Earth revolves around the sun.
2. `constellation_appearance`: Constellations appear in one place in spring and another in fall.
3. `stars_rotation`: Stars rotate diagonally in the sky.
4. `axis_widening`: Earth's axis is widened.
5. `star_fading`: Stars fade as winter approaches.

### Facts (Ground Truths):
1. `Earth revolves around sun`.
2. `Aries_Constellation` and `Sagittarius_Constellation` are sibling concepts.
3. `Interstate 24` runs diagonally from Illinois to Tennessee.
4. The Gulf of Euboea is an arm of the Aegean Sea.

### Rules (Horn Clauses / Implications):
1. If Earth revolves around sun, then constellations appear in one place in spring and another in fall: `revolves_around :- constellation_appearance`.
2. If stars rotate diagonally in the sky, then earth's axis is widened: `stars_rotation :- axis_widening`.
3. If stars fade as winter approaches, then Earth revolves around sun: `star_fading :- revolve_sund`.
